## The executor's four proposals — ruled
- **Q1 (writeRecording refuses overwrite ALWAYS; recorder skips existing keys) — ACCEPTED.**
  Wider than cluster F's words and better than them: exclusive-create by construction removes
  the flag-seam a test-only mode would have added. Re-record-requires-delete is correct
  because the key hashes the complete request — an existing file already answers it, and a
  silent replacement is a verdict moving with nothing in the diff to say so. The recorder's
  skip-and-log makes partial re-records resumable.
- **Q2 (duplicate-key check ordered before filename check) — ACCEPTED.** The ordering makes
  the shadow attack produce its own named error and gives each rule a case the other cannot
  reach — without it the second rule is untested code.
- **Q3 (`liveJudgeResponse`, a new exported name outside the alias probes) — RESIDUAL
  RE-READ AND CONFIRMED, no probe extension.** Checked in the tree: it is an
  `export function` at oracles.ts:776. The alias-probe scope protects types a SUITE can use
  to fabricate or widen its seam; this is internal machinery a suite never receives — a
  caller lying to itself, the same category registry.ts's structural-signature exception
  documents. The tree's function-vs-const convention was also weighed: the const rule exists
  where a merged overload could restore fabricated type arguments handed to TEST BODIES
  (atTest, defineEvidenceCapture); no such path exists here, so the form is immaterial.
  Ruled: residual unchanged, now for re-examined reasons rather than assumed ones.
- **Q4 (sentinel criterion ids renamed so the assertion fails on leaks, not on ids) —
  ACCEPTED, recorded.** Same defect class the cluster targets: a test failing for a reason
  other than the one it names misleads exactly like one that cannot fail. Caught on first
  run; the record now says so.

## Way-of-work findings carried to reflection (ride along in this PR)
1. A subagent that cannot resolve its parent's name reports to `main` silently — the item
   agent sleeps through its own child finishing. The completion channel needs either a
   resolvable parent name or an explicit file-based alarm the parent names at spawn time.
2. The tracked-child alarm has been unreliable for this item across every phase; the
   coordinator's named-file backstop was the working mechanism each time.
3. A read-intended reviewer (Kimi) wrote probe files into the tree mid-review and cleaned up;
   review launch prompts should state the write policy explicitly.

"C:\\WINDOWS\\System32\\WindowsPowerShell\\v1.0\\powershell.exe" -Command '$lines = Get-Content -Encoding UTF8 -LiteralPath '"'tests/at/harness/oracles.ts'; for ("'$n = 480; $n -le 710; $n++) { '"'{0}: {1}' -f "'$n, $lines[$n - 1] }' in C:\Users\nirdr\Downloads\ai4good\.claude\worktrees\agent-a39ef92872b61dcff

480: 
481: /* ---------------------------------------------------------------------- the replay store */
482: 
483: /** Where committed recordings live. Resolved from THIS file: they are harness data, not suite data. */
484: export const RECORDINGS_DIR: string = resolve(fileURLToPath(new URL('.', import.meta.url)), 'recordings');
485: 
486: /**
487:  * One recorded judge call.
488:  *
489:  * `recordedFrom` is the answer to finding F2 — "how would anyone know a committed recording came
490:  * from the judge it claims?". A synthetic entry is a legitimate thing for conformance to write
491:  * into a temp directory; it is not a legitimate thing to find in the committed store, and both
492:  * the reader and the writer refuse it there. Nothing in this file can produce a synthetic entry
493:  * that claims to be live, because `recordedFrom` is set by whoever calls the writer and the only
494:  * live caller is `record-oracles.ts`, which sets it after a real call returns.
495:  */
496: export type RecordingEntry = {
497:   key: string;
498:   voteIndex: number;
499:   recordedAt: string;
500:   recordedFrom: 'live' | 'synthetic';
501:   request: JudgeRequest;
502:   servedModel: string;
503:   stopReason: string;
504:   stopDetail?: string;
505:   text: string;
506: };
507: 
508: /**
509:  * The path as the FILESYSTEM understands it, not as the string does.
510:  *
511:  * `resolve()` alone compares text, and on the platform this repo is developed on that is the
512:  * wrong comparison: Windows filesystems are case-insensitive, so an upper-cased spelling of the
513:  * committed directory is the same physical directory and compared unequal — measured, and it is
514:  * how the write-side guard below could be walked around (Gate 2 cluster A.2). `realpathSync`
515:  * additionally collapses symlinks and junctions, so a link pointing at the store is recognised as
516:  * the store. It throws for a path that does not exist yet, which is an ordinary case for a temp
517:  * directory about to be created, so that falls back to the resolved text.
518:  */
519: function canonicalDir(dir: string): string {
520:   let canonical = resolve(dir);
521:   try {
522:     canonical = realpathSync(canonical);
523:   } catch {
524:     /* not created yet — the resolved text is the best answer available, and is enough */
525:   }
526:   return process.platform === 'win32' ? canonical.toLowerCase() : canonical;
527: }
528: 
529: /** Is this the committed store — the one directory whose entries must all be live? */
530: export function isCommittedRecordingsDir(dir: string): boolean {
531:   return canonicalDir(dir) === canonicalDir(RECORDINGS_DIR);
532: }
533: 
534: /** The one place the `${key}.json` convention lives. The reader enforces it; the writer obeys it. */
535: export function recordingFilePath(dir: string, key: string): string {
536:   return join(dir, `${key}.json`);
537: }
538: 
539: /**
540:  * Everything wrong with one recording, from the store's point of view.
541:  *
542:  * The key check is the load-bearing one and it is not decoration: the store indexes by the key the
543:  * entry DECLARES, so an entry could otherwise name any key it liked and answer a request it was
544:  * never a recording of. Re-deriving the hash from the stored request makes a hand-edited key a
545:  * refusal instead of a silent hit.
546:  */
547: export function recordingProblem(entry: RecordingEntry, opts: { committed: boolean }): string | null {
548:   if (entry === null || typeof entry !== 'object') return 'a recording must be an object';
549:   if (typeof entry.key !== 'string' || entry.key.trim() === '') return 'a recording carries no key';
550:   if (!Number.isInteger(entry.voteIndex) || entry.voteIndex < 0) {
551:     return `recording ${entry.key} carries a nonsensical vote index (${String(entry.voteIndex)})`;
552:   }
553:   if (entry.recordedFrom !== 'live' && entry.recordedFrom !== 'synthetic') {
554:     return (
555:       `recording ${entry.key} does not say how it was produced (recordedFrom=${JSON.stringify(entry.recordedFrom)}) — ` +
556:       `a recording with no provenance cannot be told from an invented one`
557:     );
558:   }
559:   if (opts.committed && entry.recordedFrom !== 'live') {
560:     return (
561:       `recording ${entry.key} is marked ${JSON.stringify(entry.recordedFrom)} and sits in the COMMITTED store, whose ` +
562:       `entries must every one be the output of a real judge call. A synthetic recording here would make the loop ` +
563:       `tier green against a verdict no judge ever gave.`
564:     );
565:   }
566:   if (entry.request === null || typeof entry.request !== 'object') return `recording ${entry.key} carries no request`;
567:   if (typeof entry.servedModel !== 'string' || entry.servedModel.trim() === '') {
568:     return `recording ${entry.key} does not say which model served it`;
569:   }
570:   if (typeof entry.stopReason !== 'string' || entry.stopReason.trim() === '') {
571:     return `recording ${entry.key} does not say why the judge stopped`;
572:   }
573:   if (typeof entry.text !== 'string') return `recording ${entry.key} carries no response text`;
574:   if (typeof entry.recordedAt !== 'string' || entry.recordedAt.trim() === '') {
575:     return `recording ${entry.key} carries no recording timestamp`;
576:   }
577:   const derived = replayKey(entry.request, entry.voteIndex);
578:   if (derived !== entry.key) {
579:     return (
580:       `recording ${entry.key} declares a key its own stored request does not hash to (${derived}) — the entry has ` +
581:       `been edited by hand, or the request was recorded under different code`
582:     );
583:   }
584:   return null;
585: }
586: 
587: /**
588:  * What the writer is given. The key, the timestamp AND THE PROVENANCE are the writer's to set.
589:  *
590:  * `recordedFrom` left this type in the Gate 2 fix round (cluster A.3): while a caller supplied it,
591:  * `recordedFrom: 'live'` was an assertion anyone could make, and the committed store's whole
592:  * defence rested on that assertion being true. It is now derived from the transport that actually
593:  * produced the bytes, so a fake cannot mint live provenance even by asking for it.
594:  */
595: export type RecordingDraft = Omit<RecordingEntry, 'key' | 'recordedAt' | 'recordedFrom'>;
596: 
597: /**
598:  * Atomic, exclusive, and provenance-derived.
599:  *
600:  * ATOMIC: written beside the target and renamed into place, so a crash mid-write cannot leave a
601:  * half-parsed recording that the store would then refuse for the wrong reason.
602:  *
603:  * EXCLUSIVE: an existing file for this key is a REFUSAL, never an overwrite (Gate 2 cluster F).
604:  * The key is a hash of the complete request, so a file already sitting there already answers this
605:  * exact request — replacing it silently is how a verdict changes with nothing to show for it. To
606:  * re-record, delete the file first and let git show what went. The existence check and the rename
607:  * are not one atomic step; two writers racing is the one case this does not cover, and it is
608:  * ruled out of scope deliberately — no key exists in CI and the recorder is a manual parent-side
609:  * tool, so a lock would be machinery for a situation the repository cannot reach.
610:  *
611:  * DERIVED: `recordedFrom` comes from the transport's own brand. ONE WRITER for both the recorder
612:  * and conformance (F2), and conformance exercising it into temp directories is what keeps the
613:  * replay path tested while the committed store is empty — but conformance's entries are now
614:  * marked `synthetic` because that is what they are.
615:  */
616: export function writeRecording(dir: string, transport: JudgeTransport, draft: RecordingDraft): RecordingEntry {
617:   const entry: RecordingEntry = {
618:     ...draft,
619:     recordedFrom: transport.kind === 'live' ? 'live' : 'synthetic',
620:     key: replayKey(draft.request, draft.voteIndex),
621:     recordedAt: new Date().toISOString(),
622:   };
623:   const problem = recordingProblem(entry, { committed: isCommittedRecordingsDir(dir) });
624:   if (problem !== null) throw new OracleError(`refusing to write a recording: ${problem}`);
625:   mkdirSync(dir, { recursive: true });
626:   const target = recordingFilePath(dir, entry.key);
627:   if (existsSync(target)) {
628:     throw new OracleError(
629:       `refusing to write a recording: ${target} already exists. The key is a hash of the complete request, so ` +
630:         `that file already answers this exact request — overwriting it would change what the loop tier replays ` +
631:         `with nothing in the diff to say a verdict moved. Delete it first if you mean to re-record.`,
632:     );
633:   }
634:   const scratch = `${target}.writing-${process.pid}`;
635:   writeFileSync(scratch, `${JSON.stringify(entry, null, 2)}\n`, 'utf8');
636:   renameSync(scratch, target);
637:   return entry;
638: }
639: 
640: /**
641:  * Every recording in a directory, indexed by key. Refuses the whole directory on any bad entry.
642:  *
643:  * TWO OF THE THREE CHECKS BELOW EXIST BECAUSE OF ONE MEASURED HOLE (Gate 2 cluster A.1). The store
644:  * indexes by the key an entry DECLARES, and a second file may declare a key the first one already
645:  * holds: `entries.set()` overwrote silently, files are read in sorted filename order, so a file
646:  * named later in the alphabet always won. A shadow recording committed beside a real one flipped
647:  * what the loop tier replayed, with no refusal anywhere — the exact quiet provenance failure the
648:  * F2 ruling exists to make impossible, arriving by a door nobody had looked at.
649:  *
650:  * So: the filename must BE the key, and a duplicate declared key refuses the whole store. Either
651:  * check alone would close the measured hole; both are here because they fail for different
652:  * reasons and a reader deserves to be told which one fired.
653:  */
654: export function readRecordings(dir: string): Map<string, RecordingEntry> {
655:   const committed = isCommittedRecordingsDir(dir);
656:   let files: string[];
657:   try {
658:     files = readdirSync(dir).filter((name) => name.endsWith('.json'));
659:   } catch {
660:     // A missing directory is an EMPTY store, not a crash: with no judge credential this item ships
661:     // the directory empty on purpose, and the useful error is the miss below, which names the key.
662:     return new Map();
663:   }
664:   const entries = new Map<string, RecordingEntry>();
665:   for (const name of files.sort()) {
666:     const path = join(dir, name);
667:     let parsed: RecordingEntry;
668:     try {
669:       parsed = JSON.parse(readFileSync(path, 'utf8')) as RecordingEntry;
670:     } catch (err) {
671:       throw new OracleError(`recording ${path} is not readable JSON — ${err instanceof Error ? err.message : String(err)}`);
672:     }
673:     const problem = recordingProblem(parsed, { committed });
674:     if (problem !== null) throw new OracleError(`refusing the replay store at ${dir}: ${problem} (${name})`);
675:     // DUPLICATE FIRST, because it is the shadow attack's own error and says the useful thing: two
676:     // files, one key, and the store about to pick one of them by filename order.
677:     if (entries.has(parsed.key)) {
678:       throw new OracleError(
679:         `refusing the replay store at ${dir}: two files declare the key ${parsed.key} (${name} is the second). ` +
680:           `Whichever sorted later would silently decide what the loop tier replays.`,
681:       );
682:     }
683:     if (name !== `${parsed.key}.json`) {
684:       throw new OracleError(
685:         `refusing the replay store at ${dir}: ${name} declares key ${parsed.key}, so it must be named ` +
686:           `${parsed.key}.json. A recording free to take any filename can be placed to sort wherever it likes.`,
687:       );
688:     }
689:     entries.set(parsed.key, parsed);
690:   }
691:   return entries;
692: }
693: 
694: /**
695:  * The loop-tier transport: committed bytes, no network, no credential.
696:  *
697:  * The index is read ONCE per instance and never written, which is what makes the oracle stateless
698:  * per harness in the sense finding F12 asked for — `createHarness()` builds a fresh one, so
699:  * nothing a test does can reach the next test through here, and there is no resource to dispose.
700:  */
701: export function createReplayTransport(dir: string): JudgeTransport {
702:   let index: Map<string, RecordingEntry> | null = null;
703:   return {
704:     kind: 'replay-fs',
705:     async send(request, voteIndex) {
706:       index ??= readRecordings(dir);
707:       const key = replayKey(request, voteIndex);
708:       const entry = index.get(key);
709:       if (!entry) {
710:         throw new OracleReplayMiss(

153: /** What a transport hands back. Deliberately bytes-and-metadata: no parsing happens out there. */
154: export type JudgeResponse = {
155:   /** where THESE BYTES came from on THIS run — never where they were originally produced */
156:   source: 'live' | 'replay';
157:   /** `response.model`: what the provider says it served, which is not the same fact as `model` */
158:   servedModel: string;
159:   stopReason: string;
160:   /** the refusal category or truncation detail, when the provider supplied one */
161:   stopDetail?: string;
162:   text: string;
163: };
164: 
165: /**
166:  * WHAT KIND OF THING PRODUCED THESE BYTES. Not decoration: two separate rules are enforced off
167:  * this brand, and neither could be enforced without it (Gate 2 cluster B, and cluster A.3).
168:  *
169:  *   - the capability factory refuses a live transport at loop tier and a filesystem-replay
170:  *     transport above it, so the injectable seam cannot defeat the tier ruling from outside;
171:  *   - the recorder DERIVES a recording's provenance from the transport that produced it, so
172:  *     `recordedFrom: 'live'` is no longer something a caller can simply assert.
173:  *
174:  * `'fake'` is legal at every tier on purpose. Conformance fakes are the instrument the tier rules
175:  * are proved WITH; barring them would leave the rules untestable, which is a worse trade than
176:  * allowing an obviously-labelled fake. What a fake can never do is mint a live-provenance
177:  * recording — that follows from the derivation above rather than from anyone's discipline.
178:  */
179: export type TransportKind = 'replay-fs' | 'live' | 'fake';
180: 
181: /**
182:  * The injectable seam. Both real implementations below satisfy it, and so do the fakes in
183:  * `oracles.selftest.ts` — which is what lets the entire conformance wall run offline, with a
184:  * transport that throws if it is touched standing in for "the network was not reached".
185:  */
186: export type JudgeTransport = {
187:   kind: TransportKind;
188:   send(request: JudgeRequest, voteIndex: number): Promise<JudgeResponse>;
189: };
190: 
1118:  * of producing a recording that claims live provenance.
1119:  */
1120: export function createOracleCapability(opts: {
1121:   tier: Tier;
1122:   config: ConfigRegistry;
1123:   recordingsDir?: string;
1124:   transport?: JudgeTransport;
1125: }): Capability<SemanticOracle> {
1126:   const votes = readJudgeVotes(opts.config);
1127:   if (opts.tier === 'loop') {
1128:     const transport = opts.transport ?? createReplayTransport(opts.recordingsDir ?? RECORDINGS_DIR);
1129:     if (transport.kind === 'live') {
1130:       throw new OracleError(
1131:         'refusing to build a loop-tier oracle on a live transport: the loop tier is bit-deterministic because it ' +
1132:           'replays committed recordings, and a live call there would report today\'s answer under yesterday\'s ' +
1133:           'expectations while the capability ledger still called it a stand-in.',
1134:       );
1135:     }
1136:     return standInCapability('oracles.judge', createSemanticOracle({ transport, votes }));
1137:   }
1138:   const transport = opts.transport ?? createLiveTransport();
1139:   if (transport.kind === 'replay-fs') {
1140:     throw new OracleError(
1141:       `refusing to build a ${opts.tier}-tier oracle on a filesystem replay transport: above loop the oracle is ` +
1142:         `registered REAL, and a real capability answering from committed bytes is the gate grading a recording ` +
1143:         `while reporting that it stubbed nothing.`,
1144:     );
1145:   }
1146:   return realCapability('oracles.judge', createSemanticOracle({ transport, votes }));
1147: }

764:  *
765:  * The ordering is the fix for Gate 2 terra 4, and the reason it is worth a named function is that
766:  * ordering is the only thing being asserted, so it needs somewhere a test can reach without a
767:  * client. Content was previously filtered and joined before anything looked at why the model
768:  * stopped: on a refusal or a truncation that is work done on material the caller is about to
769:  * throw away, and it reads as though the text mattered when the call did not finish. Now nothing
770:  * touches `content` unless the message says it finished.
771:  *
772:  * The caller still decides what a non-`end_turn` stop MEANS — `judge()` raises the typed error, so
773:  * a REPLAYED refusal is refused on the same path as a live one. This function only declines to
774:  * look at bytes it has been told not to trust.
775:  */
776: export function liveJudgeResponse(message: LiveMessage): JudgeResponse {
777:   const stopReason = message.stop_reason ?? '<none reported>';
778:   const stopDetail = message.stop_details?.category ?? undefined;
779:   const detail = stopDetail ? { stopDetail } : {};
780:   if (stopReason !== 'end_turn') {
781:     return { source: 'live', servedModel: message.model, stopReason, ...detail, text: '' };
782:   }
783:   const text = message.content
784:     .filter((block): block is { type: 'text'; text: string } => (block as { type?: unknown })?.type === 'text')
785:     .map((block) => block.text)
786:     .join('');
787:   return { source: 'live', servedModel: message.model, stopReason, ...detail, text };
788: }
789: 
790: export function createLiveTransport(): JudgeTransport {
791:   let client: Anthropic | null = null;
792:   return {
793:     kind: 'live',
794:     async send(request, voteIndex) {
795:       // Every vote sends the IDENTICAL request; they differ only by being separate samples. The
796:       // index reaches the key, not the wire.
797:       void voteIndex;
798:       const apiKey = process.env.AT_JUDGE_API_KEY?.trim();
799:       if (!apiKey) {
800:         throw new OracleUnavailable(
801:           'the semantic oracle has no judge credential in this process. AT_JUDGE_API_KEY is read only by the ' +
802:             'PARENT-side recorder and smoke scripts and is deliberately not passed into any test child: how a ' +
803:             'credential should reach a child at integration tier — a broker, a scoped key, something else — is a ' +
804:             'decision deferred to the slice that makes the integration tier real, taken with its first consuming ' +
805:             'run rather than guessed at now (AI4DEV-20 plan §2, ruling F8). At loop tier the oracle replays ' +
806:             'committed recordings and needs no credential at all.',
807:         );
808:       }
809:       if (!client) {
810:         const { default: AnthropicClient } = await import('@anthropic-ai/sdk');
811:         client = new AnthropicClient({ apiKey });
812:       }
813:       const message = await client.messages.create({
814:         model: request.model,
815:         max_tokens: request.maxTokens,
816:         system: request.system,
817:         messages: request.messages,
818:         output_config: {
819:           effort: request.effort,
820:           format: { type: 'json_schema', schema: request.outputSchema },
821:         },
822:       });
823:       return liveJudgeResponse(message);
824:     },
825:   };
826: }
827: 
828: /* --------------------------------------------------------------- parsing and aggregation */
829: 
830: function answersProblem(rubric: Rubric, answers: JudgeAnswer[]): string | null {
850:   }
851:   const unanswered = rubric.criteria.filter((criterion) => !byId.has(criterion.id)).map((criterion) => criterion.id);
852:   if (unanswered.length) {
853:     return `the judge left criteri${unanswered.length === 1 ? 'on' : 'a'} ${unanswered.join(', ')} unanswered`;
854:   }
855:   return null;
856: }
857: 
858: /**
859:  * The property names the verdict schema allows, per shape. Kept beside the schema they mirror.
860:  *
861:  * `VERDICT_SCHEMA` says `additionalProperties: false` and the server enforces that; local
862:  * re-validation used to ignore it entirely, so the same bytes could be accepted here and rejected
863:  * there (Gate 2 terra 5). Two consequences, both bad: a REPLAYED response could carry fields a
864:  * live one never could, and an answer object carrying both `holds` and `value` — a judge that
865:  * hedged rather than answered — was silently read as whichever field its declared kind wanted.
866:  */
867: const ALLOWED_VERDICT_KEYS = ['answers'];
868: const ALLOWED_ANSWER_KEYS: Record<string, string[]> = {
869:   semantic: ['criterionId', 'kind', 'holds', 'evidence'],
870:   extraction: ['criterionId', 'kind', 'value', 'evidence'],
871: };
872: 
873: function unknownKeys(value: object, allowed: string[]): string[] {
874:   return Object.keys(value).filter((key) => !allowed.includes(key));
875: }
876: 
877: /** Local re-validation of one vote's structured output. Anything unexpected is an error, never a pass. */
878: export function parseJudgeAnswers(rubric: Rubric, text: string): JudgeAnswer[] {
879:   let parsed: unknown;
880:   try {
881:     parsed = JSON.parse(text);
882:   } catch (err) {
883:     throw new OracleError(
884:       `the judge's output is not JSON (${err instanceof Error ? err.message : String(err)}). The verdict schema is ` +
885:         `enforced server-side, so unparseable output means the response was truncated or the request was not the ` +
886:         `one this code believes it sent.`,
887:     );
888:   }
889:   if (parsed === null || typeof parsed !== 'object' || Array.isArray(parsed)) {
890:     throw new OracleError("the judge's output is not a verdict object");
891:   }
892:   const surplus = unknownKeys(parsed, ALLOWED_VERDICT_KEYS);
893:   if (surplus.length) {
894:     throw new OracleError(
895:       `the judge's output carries ${surplus.join(', ')}, which the verdict schema does not allow. The server rejects ` +
896:         `these; accepting them here would let a replayed response say things a live one never could.`,
897:     );
898:   }
899:   const answersRaw = (parsed as { answers?: unknown }).answers;
900:   if (!Array.isArray(answersRaw)) throw new OracleError("the judge's output carries no `answers` array");
901:   const answers: JudgeAnswer[] = [];
902:   for (const raw of answersRaw as Record<string, unknown>[]) {
903:     if (raw === null || typeof raw !== 'object') throw new OracleError('the judge returned an answer that is not an object');
904:     const criterionId = raw.criterionId;
905:     const evidence = raw.evidence;
906:     if (typeof criterionId !== 'string' || criterionId.trim() === '') {
907:       throw new OracleError('the judge returned an answer with no criterion id');
908:     }
909:     if (typeof evidence !== 'string' || evidence.trim() === '') {
910:       throw new OracleError(`the judge returned no evidence for criterion ${JSON.stringify(criterionId)}`);
911:     }
912:     if (raw.kind === 'semantic' || raw.kind === 'extraction') {
913:       const extra = unknownKeys(raw, ALLOWED_ANSWER_KEYS[raw.kind]!);
914:       if (extra.length) {
915:         throw new OracleError(
916:           `the judge's ${raw.kind} answer for ${JSON.stringify(criterionId)} carries ${extra.join(', ')}, which the ` +
917:             `verdict schema does not allow. An answer holding both a judgement and a value is a judge that hedged ` +
918:             `rather than answered, and reading whichever field its declared kind wanted would hide that.`,
919:         );
920:       }
921:     }
922:     if (raw.kind === 'semantic') {
923:       if (typeof raw.holds !== 'boolean') {
924:         throw new OracleError(`the judge's semantic answer for ${JSON.stringify(criterionId)} carries no boolean \`holds\``);
925:       }
926:       answers.push({ criterionId, kind: 'semantic', holds: raw.holds, evidence });
927:       continue;
928:     }
929:     if (raw.kind === 'extraction') {
930:       if (typeof raw.value !== 'number' || !Number.isFinite(raw.value)) {
931:         throw new OracleError(
932:           `the judge's extraction answer for ${JSON.stringify(criterionId)} carries no finite \`value\` (${String(raw.value)})`,
933:         );
934:       }
935:       answers.push({ criterionId, kind: 'extraction', value: raw.value, evidence });
936:       continue;
937:     }
938:     throw new OracleError(`the judge returned an answer of unknown kind ${JSON.stringify(raw.kind)}`);
939:   }
940:   const problem = answersProblem(rubric, answers);

96: export type JudgeEffort = 'low' | 'medium' | 'high' | 'xhigh' | 'max';
97: 
98: /* --------------------------------------------------------------------------- typed failures */
99: 
100: /** Every way this capability can decline to answer. It declines loudly; it never passes. */
101: export class OracleError extends Error {
102:   constructor(message: string) {
103:     super(message);
104:     this.name = 'OracleError';
105:   }
106: }
107: 
108: /**
109:  * The replay store had no entry for a key. Carries the key, because the ONLY useful next step is
110:  * to record that exact request — and because a miss must be visibly different from a live
111:  * transport with no credential, which is how conformance proves loop tier never falls through.
112:  */
113: export class OracleReplayMiss extends OracleError {
114:   constructor(
115:     readonly key: string,
116:     message: string,
117:   ) {
118:     super(message);
119:     this.name = 'OracleReplayMiss';
120:   }
121: }
122: 
123: /** The live judge cannot run in this process — see the message, which names the deferred boundary. */
124: export class OracleUnavailable extends OracleError {
125:   constructor(message: string) {
126:     super(message);
127:     this.name = 'OracleUnavailable';
128:   }
129: }
130: 
131: /* ------------------------------------------------------------------------------ the request */
132: 
133: /**
134:  * THE COMPLETE RENDERED REQUEST — every input that can change what the judge answers, in one
135:  * serializable object, because that object is what gets hashed (F6).
136:  *
137:  * `rubricId` and `rubricVersion` ride along even though the prompt already contains them in
138:  * prose: a machine-readable copy lets a recording be identified by a human and, more usefully,
139:  * makes a rubric renamed-but-otherwise-identical produce a different key.
140:  */
141: export type JudgeRequest = {
142:   promptVersion: string;
143:   rubricId: string;
144:   rubricVersion: string;
145:   model: string;
146:   maxTokens: number;
147:   effort: JudgeEffort;
148:   outputSchema: Record<string, unknown>;
390:     }
391:   }
392:   return null;
393: }
394: 
395: /* ------------------------------------------------------------------- rendering and hashing */
396: 
397: function renderCriterion(criterion: RubricCriterion): string {
398:   const head = `CRITERION ${criterion.id} (${criterion.kind})\n  ${criterion.statement.trim()}`;
399:   if (criterion.kind === 'semantic') return head;
400:   const parts = [`  extract: ${criterion.extract.what.trim()}`];
401:   if (criterion.extract.unit) parts.push(`  unit: ${criterion.extract.unit.trim()}`);
402:   if (criterion.extract.normalization) parts.push(`  normalization: ${criterion.extract.normalization.trim()}`);
403:   return `${head}\n${parts.join('\n')}`;
404: }
405: 
406: /**
407:  * THE COMPARATOR IS NOT IN THIS PROMPT and that omission is the design. Telling the judge
408:  * "expected 4200, tolerance 420" invites it to answer the comparison rather than perform the
409:  * extraction, and then the arithmetic is back inside the generation where its instability came
410:  * from. The judge is asked for a number; `compareExtraction()` below owns the threshold.
411:  */
412: export function renderSystemPrompt(rubric: Rubric): string {
413:   return [
414:     'You are judging whether supplied text satisfies a fixed rubric, as part of an automated acceptance test.',
415:     '',
416:     'Answer only from the material below. Do not use outside knowledge about the product it came from.',
417:     'Answer every criterion exactly once, keyed by its id, and answer no criterion that is not listed.',
418:     '',
419:     'For a criterion of kind "semantic", set `holds` to whether its statement is true of the material.',
420:     'For a criterion of kind "extraction", set `value` to the number the criterion names, under the unit and',
421:     'normalization it gives. Do not decide whether that number is acceptable: a threshold is applied to it in',
422:     'code, outside this request.',
423:     '',
424:     'For every criterion, set `evidence` to a short verbatim quote from the material. Where your answer rests on',
425:     'something being absent, quote the passage where it would have appeared.',
426:     '',
427:     `RUBRIC ${rubric.id} (version ${rubric.version})`,
428:     '',
429:     rubric.criteria.map(renderCriterion).join('\n\n'),
430:   ].join('\n');
431: }
432: 
433: /** Slots are rendered in the RUBRIC's declared order, never the caller object's key order. */
434: export function renderMaterial(rubric: Rubric, material: Record<string, string>): string {
435:   return rubric.materialSlots
436:     .map((slot) => `<material slot="${slot}">\n${material[slot]}\n</material>`)
437:     .join('\n\n');
438: }
439: 
440: export function renderJudgeRequest(rubric: Rubric, material: Record<string, string>): JudgeRequest {
441:   return {
442:     promptVersion: PROMPT_VERSION,
443:     rubricId: rubric.id,
444:     rubricVersion: rubric.version,
445:     model: JUDGE_MODEL,
446:     maxTokens: JUDGE_MAX_TOKENS,
447:     effort: JUDGE_EFFORT,
448:     outputSchema: VERDICT_SCHEMA,
449:     system: renderSystemPrompt(rubric),
450:     messages: [{ role: 'user', content: renderMaterial(rubric, material) }],
451:   };
452: }
453: 
454: /**
455:  * Key order is not part of a request's meaning, so it must not be part of its hash — otherwise a
456:  * refactor that reorders a literal invalidates every recording for no reason at all.
457:  */
458: export function canonicalJson(value: unknown): string {
459:   if (value === null || typeof value !== 'object') return JSON.stringify(value) ?? 'null';
460:   if (Array.isArray(value)) return `[${value.map(canonicalJson).join(',')}]`;
461:   const entries = Object.entries(value as Record<string, unknown>)
462:     .filter(([, entryValue]) => entryValue !== undefined)
463:     .sort(([left], [right]) => (left < right ? -1 : left > right ? 1 : 0));
464:   return `{${entries.map(([key, entryValue]) => `${JSON.stringify(key)}:${canonicalJson(entryValue)}`).join(',')}}`;
465: }

458: export function canonicalJson(value: unknown): string {
459:   if (value === null || typeof value !== 'object') return JSON.stringify(value) ?? 'null';
460:   if (Array.isArray(value)) return `[${value.map(canonicalJson).join(',')}]`;
461:   const entries = Object.entries(value as Record<string, unknown>)
462:     .filter(([, entryValue]) => entryValue !== undefined)
463:     .sort(([left], [right]) => (left < right ? -1 : left > right ? 1 : 0));
464:   return `{${entries.map(([key, entryValue]) => `${JSON.stringify(key)}:${canonicalJson(entryValue)}`).join(',')}}`;
465: }
466: 
467: /**
468:  * THE REPLAY KEY IS THE WHOLE REQUEST, PLUS THE VOTE INDEX.
469:  *
470:  * Model id, max tokens, effort, the output schema, the fully rendered system and user text with
471:  * every material value in it — all of it. Any code change that alters what the judge is asked
472:  * produces a different key, so recordings go stale by construction rather than by discipline
473:  * (F6). The vote index is in the key because the k votes send an identical request and are
474:  * distinguished only by being separate samples; without it, replay would answer all k votes with
475:  * one recording and the majority would be unanimous by accident.
476:  */
477: export function replayKey(request: JudgeRequest, voteIndex: number): string {
478:   return createHash('sha256').update(canonicalJson({ request, voteIndex }), 'utf8').digest('hex');
479: }

947:  * judge behaving the same way twice: given the same extracted number, this returns the same answer
948:  * forever.
949:  */
950: export function compareExtraction(criterion: ExtractionCriterion, value: number): boolean {
951:   const compare = criterion.compare;
952:   if (compare.op === 'numeric_within_tolerance') {
953:     // INCLUSIVE at the boundary: a tolerance of 0.5 means half a unit either way is acceptable,
954:     // and "acceptable" that excludes its own stated limit is a different tolerance.
955:     return Math.abs(value - compare.expected) <= compare.tolerance;
956:   }
957:   if (!Number.isInteger(value)) {
958:     // A COUNT IS NOT ROUNDED. "2.5 occurrences" is the judge failing to count, and rounding it to
959:     // whichever side happens to pass is the guess this harness exists to refuse.
960:     throw new OracleError(
961:       `criterion ${JSON.stringify(criterion.id)} counts occurrences and the judge extracted ${value}, which is not a ` +
962:         `whole number of them`,
963:     );
964:   }
965:   return value >= compare.minimum;
966: }
967: 
968: /**
969:  * Strict majority, and a TIE IS A FAIL.
970:  *
971:  * Exported so a test can drive it with a tally the rest of the system cannot produce. While this
972:  * lived inline, the odd-vote-count validation and the `>` were each other's only witness: swap the
973:  * `>` for `>=` and nothing anywhere goes red, because k is always odd so no tie ever reaches it.
974:  * Two guards protecting each other is one regression away from neither (Gate 2 cluster G). If the
975:  * odd-k refusal ever weakens, this decides an even split the safe way rather than the quiet way.
976:  */
977: export function majorityPass(passCount: number, votes: number): boolean {
978:   return passCount * 2 > votes;
979: }
980:

611:   it('passes on 2-1 and fails on 1-2, and reports the tally either way', async () => {

613:       { alpha: true, beta: true },

619:       pass: true,

630:       pass: false,

637:     // reaches the majority rule — which means swapping its `>` for `>=` breaks nothing anywhere and
638:     // no test goes red. The odd-k validation and the strict comparison were each other's only
639:     // guard, and two guards protecting each other is one regression away from neither. Driving the
640:     // rule directly with an even tally is the only way to witness which way a tie would fall.
641:     expect(majorityPass(1, 2), 'a 1-1 tie passed').toBe(false);
642:     expect(majorityPass(2, 4), 'a 2-2 tie passed').toBe(false);
643:     expect(majorityPass(50, 100), 'an even split passed').toBe(false);
644:     // And it still decides the cases that do occur.
645:     expect(majorityPass(2, 3)).toBe(true);
646:     expect(majorityPass(1, 3)).toBe(false);
647:     expect(majorityPass(3, 5)).toBe(true);
648:     expect(majorityPass(2, 5)).toBe(false);
649:     expect(majorityPass(0, 1)).toBe(false);
650:     expect(majorityPass(1, 1)).toBe(true);

loop/items/AI4DEV-20/gate1-stderr.txt:5380:8. [severity MAJOR] — §4d credential path — Passing `AT_JUDGE_API_KEY` to the Vitest child materially weakens the current leak model: the tree promises that provider secrets cannot reach tests, while the spawned test process inherits stdout/stderr directly and could print or misuse the key. Every test in every non-loop requirement receives it, not only semantic-oracle calls. Evidence: `.env.example:7-18`; `runner.ts:113-121,163-169,1121-1139`; `plan.md:165-172`. Fix: keep the key outside Vitest—prefer a bounded parent-side judge/recorder broker—or require an explicit, narrowly scoped live-oracle mode with a limited key and update the threat model.

30: ## What is unproved, stated plainly
31: 
32: 1. **That a live call succeeds at all.** The request shape is compile-checked against
33:    `@anthropic-ai/sdk@0.115.0` — the pinned model id, `max_tokens`, `output_config.effort` and
34:    `output_config.format` are assembled through that SDK's own parameter types, so a shape the SDK
35:    rejects fails `bun run typecheck`. That is a check of the SHAPE, not of the behaviour. Whether
36:    the judge returns a verdict matching `VERDICT_SCHEMA`, whether `stop_reason` is `end_turn`,
37:    whether the prompt elicits usable extractions — all unknown.
38: 2. **The stability bound.** Finding F1 accepted the k-vote majority argument only on condition the
39:    stability be measured. It has not been. `record-oracles.ts` is the instrument; it has not been
40:    pointed at anything.
41: 3. **The effort pin.** `low` is PROVISIONAL and labelled so in `oracles.ts`. The sweep that would
42:    settle it needs a consuming evaluation, which does not exist either.
43: 4. **The SDK under bun.** The dependency installs and type-checks; it has not been executed.
44: 
45: ## The proof point
46: 
47: The first consuming suite's integration run is where all four become answerable, and that is the
48: same slice that has to decide how a judge credential reaches a test child. Until then the loop
49: tier is honest about having nothing to replay: it fails loudly with the key it wanted rather than
50: going live, and the committed store refuses any entry that cannot show live provenance.
51: 
52: **To fill the gap**, parent-side, on a machine with a key:
53:

56: bun tests/at/harness/record-oracles.ts
57: ```
58: 
59: That records k votes per example specimen, repeats the full verdict five times to count flips,
60: overwrites this file with the measurement, and leaves the recordings to be committed alongside it.

90:  * verdict is a recording nobody should keep: it would replay as an error forever, and the loop tier
91:  * would inherit a failure that only ever happened once.

93: function recordingTransport(rubric: Rubric, record: boolean): JudgeTransport {
94:   const live = createLiveTransport();
95:   return {
96:     // 'live', because that is what it IS: the live transport with a recording side effect. The
97:     // brand is not a label anyone chooses for convenience — `writeRecording` derives a recording's
98:     // provenance from it, so this is the single point at which live provenance can be created at
99:     // all, and it sits directly on top of a real call (Gate 2 cluster A.3).
100:     kind: 'live',
101:     async send(request, voteIndex) {
102:       const response: JudgeResponse = await live.send(request, voteIndex);
103:       if (response.stopReason !== 'end_turn') {
104:         throw new Error(
105:           `vote ${voteIndex} did not finish: ${response.stopReason}${response.stopDetail ? ` (${response.stopDetail})` : ''}`,
106:         );
107:       }
108:       // Throws if the output is not a verdict for THIS rubric. Nothing unvalidated is stored.
109:       parseJudgeAnswers(rubric, response.text);
110:       if (record) {
111:         // The writer refuses to overwrite, so an existing recording is checked for here and
112:         // reported rather than allowed to abort a whole run — a re-record after a partial failure
113:         // has to be able to pick up where it stopped.
114:         const key = replayKey(request, voteIndex);
115:         if (existsSync(recordingFilePath(RECORDINGS_DIR, key))) {
116:           console.log(`    vote ${voteIndex} is already recorded (${key.slice(0, 16)}…) — keeping the existing entry`);
117:         } else {
118:           const entry = writeRecording(RECORDINGS_DIR, live, {
119:             voteIndex,
120:             request,
121:             servedModel: response.servedModel,
122:             stopReason: response.stopReason,
123:             ...(response.stopDetail === undefined ? {} : { stopDetail: response.stopDetail }),
124:             text: response.text,
125:           });
